# Findings register

One row per finding ever raised by any cycle. The source of truth for CLV Check 5. Cycle files hold the *why*; this file holds the *closure*.

### CLV Check 5 — the mechanism (POINTER, not a copy — repaired 2026-08-30)

**The mechanism is `scripts/clv_findings.py`. This section describes it and does not restate it.**

```sh
scripts/clv_findings.py                 # human-readable report; exit 0 = pass, 1 = fail, 2 = bad input
scripts/clv_findings.py --limit 0       # every row of every failing bucket
scripts/clv_findings.py --json          # machine-readable
python3 -m unittest discover -s scripts -p 'test_clv_findings.py'   # its 18 synthetic self-tests
```

**Why this section no longer contains the algorithm.** It used to hold an awk one-liner, and
`scripts/clv-lite.sh` held a hand-made copy of the same one-liner, with a comment in the shell
copy asking whoever edited this block to remember to re-sync the other. Two copies of a predicate
are two predicates, and the gate's verdict depended on which one you happened to run. There is now
exactly one implementation, it lives in `scripts/`, and — closing the "Known residual" this section
used to carry — it has a companion test file the way `ledger_drift_lint.py`/TEST-017 do.

**The five concepts, kept apart on purpose.** Conflating any two of them is how this register
produced a clean gate over a live blocker:

| concept | question it answers | unreadable ⇒ |
|---|---|---|
| malformed row | can the row be read at all? | **FAIL** |
| severity | how bad is it? | **FAIL** |
| disposition | is work still owed? | **FAIL** |
| effect set (`BLOCKS: {…}`) | what does it block? | an INDEPENDENT axis |
| outstanding | blocking severity **and** no closing disposition | **FAIL** |

`OUTSTANDING` is computed from severity and disposition ONLY. **Writing a `BLOCKS: {…}` set onto a
row closes nothing and moves nothing out of `OUTSTANDING`** — an effect set says what a finding
blocks, a disposition says whether anyone still owes work on it, and they are separately true or
false. A gate whose actions cannot change its own pass criteria is not a gate, and one previous
NEXT_GATE was written on the opposite belief.

**What changed, and in which direction.** Every change makes MORE things fail, never fewer:

- *Malformed rows were fail-OPEN.* 28 rows split into the wrong number of cells and were counted
  `NEEDS-CLASSIFICATION` — "reported, not blocking" — while several of them literally begin
  `**BLOCKING — …**`. A 29th was worse: `ORCH-009` had seven columns AND no trailing pipe, which
  splits into exactly the field count a well-formed six-column row produces, so the old predicate
  read it as valid and took its severity from the wrong cell. All 29 are repaired (see ROW
  OVERFLOW below) and an unreadable row is now a hard failure.
- *Prose severities were fail-OPEN.* The old predicate was "if the severity cell does not start
  with `blocking`, skip this row". A cell reading `The three completion slots' TAIL …` does not
  start with `blocking`, so the row left the check entirely. That is not a finding that the row is
  harmless; it is the absence of any finding. Such rows are now `UNKNOWN-SEVERITY` and they fail.
- *Prose dispositions were read by accident*, as open or closed depending only on which word they
  happened to begin with. They are now `UNKNOWN-DISPOSITION` and they fail.
- *Vocabulary*, unchanged from VAL-018's ruling and carried across deliberately: matching is
  case-insensitive on the cell's LEADING token, so `FIXED 2026-08-03 (mitigated per DEC-020…)`
  counts as dispositioned, and `blocking-for-clean-build` / `was-blocking-for-production` count as
  blocking. `fix-now` remains a CLOSURE — its convention here is `fix-now` plus the actual
  resolution in `resolved-by` — because re-reading it as open would silently re-open 36 historical
  rows on a vocabulary opinion rather than on evidence.
- *`NEEDS-DISPOSITION`* (added 2026-08-23 when B-R028-17 exposed the hole: a finding that may well
  be blocking, but whose severity the orchestrator declined to set unilaterally, was invisible)
  now FAILS rather than being reported-and-passed. Declining to classify must be visible, and an
  undetermined severity is an undetermined gate. This also retires ORCH-041, whose defect was that
  the old branch short-circuited before the disposition check so the bucket could never self-clear.

### ROW OVERFLOW — what `**[overflow col N …]**` means

The register schema has SIX columns. Twenty-three rows had been written with a seventh (two with an
eighth and ninth) holding extra narrative. **Every Markdown renderer DROPS a cell beyond the header
count**, so that text was already invisible in any rendered view of this file and unaddressable by
any positional mechanism. Those cells were merged into `resolved-by`, each introduced by
`**[overflow col N — preserved verbatim …]**`. Nothing was deleted, rewritten or summarised: the
repair was verified by a content-stream invariant — every non-whitespace character of the original
row is present in the repaired row — on all 29 rows. Six further rows carried an unescaped `|`
inside a cell (mostly a C++ `||` inside backticks; GFM does not let a code span protect a pipe, so
`\|` is the only escape) and those pipes are now escaped.

**Do not add a seventh column.** Put the narrative in `resolved-by`.

**Current run (2026-09-01, AFTER the A3-R027-F8 closure), over 364 rows:**
**0 MALFORMED · 0 UNKNOWN-SEVERITY · 0 UNKNOWN-DISPOSITION · 0 NEEDS-DISPOSITION · 27 OUTSTANDING
· 337 OK**, and **0 of the 27 outstanding rows carry no effect set** (`MISSING-EFFECT = 0`). (2026-08-30: 34/330.)
Reproduce with `scripts/clv_findings.py`; exit code is 1, because `OUTSTANDING` is non-empty.

**`OUTSTANDING` ROSE 15 → 34, and that is the gate WORKING, not a regression.** It was 15 only
because 114 rows carried a severity no mechanism could read and 8 carried an unreadable
disposition — those rows were unjudged, not harmless. Gate 1A transcribed each cell's severity and
disposition from the sentence in that row's own body that already established it, keeping the
original text after the prefix; nothing was re-adjudicated and no row's meaning was changed. The
arithmetic of the change: −3 (ORACLE-F1, A3-R028c-F1, A3-R028c-F2, adjudicated on re-executed
evidence — see [[ORCH-050]]), −1 (ORCH-050 itself, resolved by that adjudication), +23 rows that
were already blocking and open and are now legible to the mechanism.

**Effect sets are NOT dispositions.** Gate 1B wrote an explicit `BLOCKS: {…}` set onto all 27
outstanding rows that lacked one, read off each row's OWN recorded harm, with scope PROVEN rather
than assumed (`references/orchestration.md`) — so an unproven-scope row names its raising TASK, never
a global block. `OUTSTANDING` stayed at exactly 34 across that edit, which is [[LSN-083]] executed
rather than asserted.

Historical runs, kept for provenance and NOT a current status: the 2026-08-30 gate-repair pass
reported 0 / 114 / 8 / 0 / 15 / 227; 2026-08-24 reported 11 OUTSTANDING / 27 NEEDS-CLASSIFICATION;
2026-08-23 reported 8 / 27 / 1. The last two came from the fail-open predicate and are not
comparable with the ones above.

### REQ-028 Option C disposition override — 2026-08-22  (HISTORICAL NARRATIVE — NO CURRENT AUTHORITY)

**RELABELLED 2026-08-30 by Gate 1A, closing [[ORCH-050]]. This block is NOT a disposition source and
carries no verdict.** It was written as a second disposition source for rows whose own cells said
something else, which is exactly the defect ORCH-050 records: two statements of one finding's
disposition, in one file, disagreeing. Every verdict below has been moved INTO the disposition cell
of the row it is about, with that row's original text preserved verbatim after it, and each move was
made only after the cited evidence was RE-EXECUTED at `37710370e` (TEST-128; TEST-129 across three
completion channels and both drivers; `GC_FUZZ_SEED=447` — all PASS on BOTH `offscreen` and
`minimal`) and the prescribed fixes were located in the shipped source. **Read the row, not this
block.** Kept, unedited below, as dated provenance for how those verdicts were reached:

- **A3-R028c-F1 — RESOLVED.** TEST-128 was RED on the stale completion relabelling the restarted row, then GREEN after operation-keyed records survived Refresh with a null row.
- **A3-R028c-F2 — RESOLVED.** All three completion-tail rows and both parseable-driver rows in TEST-129 were independently RED, then GREEN after batch-generation checks; seed 32 passes on both backends.
- **ORACLE-F1 — RESOLVED.** The common stale-frame generation mechanism closes the held-row route; seed 447 passes on both backends under ASan.
- **A3-R028c-F3/A3-R028c-F8 — RESOLVED.** DEC-036 Option C removed scalar/capped retirement; TEST-126 now directly enforces dispatches = matched completions + outstanding records on every generated run.
- **New A3 attack-the-fix blockers — RESOLVED before acceptance.** SixCycle now reads identity from the actual sender reply; syncNext/uploadNext recheck generation after parseable openRideFile suspension; TEST-126 consumes the diagnostic accessor; the queued test callback no longer captures a dead stack local. Full offscreen is 78/0 and `garmin-fast` 25/0.
- **A3 recheck residual — OPEN, non-blocking.** SixCycle's sender-bound fix is source-reviewed and application-build verified, but no unit test crosses its real two-`QNetworkReply` reversed-completion boundary. Add that provider-specific integration test in the Cloud-adapter hardening slice.
- **A3 boundary residual — OPEN, non-blocking.** The `quint64` allocator skips zero but has no forced-wrap/live-ID collision policy or test. This requires roughly 2^64 allocations while the colliding record remains live and is not release-blocking; define collision retry and a forced-boundary seam before changing allocator semantics.
- **A3 recheck oracle residual — RESOLVED.** TEST-126 prose now describes direct observation and completion helpers resample `outstandingTransferCount()` after production removal; focused offscreen/minimal each pass 6/0.

REQ-028(c) remains partial for the independent sorting/positional-driver slice; this override does not close that defect.

| ORCH-062 | Inspector/REQ-NF-i18n-001 builder supervision, T-208 (2026-09-12) | non-blocking — tooling/process false-positive, not a code defect | THE anti_duplication_guard.py HOOK (same hook as [[ORCH-058]]) MISPARSES A BARE SHELL REDIRECT (`2>`, `2>/dev/null`) OR A LOOP'S TRAILING NUMERIC VALUE AS A NEW-FILE-CREATION TARGET, BLOCKING ROUTINE READ-ONLY BUILDER COMMANDS PENDING MANUAL APPROVAL | OPEN, non-blocking, {TASK:hook-tooling}. Recurred 6x in ~50 minutes during T-208 builder work; each instance manually approved by the Inspector after confirming the underlying command was read-only (`clang-format --dry-run`, guard-script reads) or an in-scope edit to files the builder already owned this session. Not fixed here — same "infrastructure, not this REQ's scope" rule as [[ORCH-058]] | Sibling in kind to [[ORCH-058]] — same hook, different detection path (new-file-creation regex vs. snapshot-restore-suffix regex); a 4th distinct root cause under [[LSN-036]]'s standing class. Root cause: the guard's new-file heuristic treats the token immediately after a bare `>`-family redirect (incl. `2>`) or a `for ln in … N; do` loop's trailing literal as a filename to check against the WIKI MAP, when neither is a write target. Per LSN-036's own second-order rule (prefer shipping the fix upstream over a local patch a reinstall reverts), any fix belongs in the `quality-gated-dev-workflow` package upstream. No REQ allocated; flagged for whoever next owns hook maintenance. LSN-036 miss recorded. |
| ORCH-063 | Inspector/B-STAGE9-16 Part B repair round 1 builder supervision (2026-09-16), `garmin_inspector_v1_26`. Hit within four minutes of the Inspector succession, while the builder was repairing R1-F1 — the wake caught the pane as `blocked` and the dialog was read, classified, and approved rather than routed around, per [[LSN-036]]'s own rule | non-blocking — tooling/process false-positive, not a code defect | Guard misses that `mkdir -p` made the dir its `cd` enters | OPEN, non-blocking, {TASK:hook-tooling}. A FIFTH distinct root cause under [[LSN-036]]'s standing class, and the first that is NOT a regex/parsing bug — it is a missing state update in the guard's own sequential walk. `_resolve_cd()` (`.claude/hooks/anti_duplication_guard.py:293-329`) ends with `return ap if os.path.isdir(ap) else None`, evaluated at HOOK time, i.e. BEFORE the command runs. So for the extremely ordinary scratch-probe shape `mkdir -p /tmp/probe && cd /tmp/probe && git init -q . && ...`, the `cd` target does not exist yet, `_resolve_cd` correctly returns None by its own documented contract, the effective cwd for the following relative operand (`.`) becomes unknown, and the run stops on the `unresolved_ask` message at `:609-618`. The guard's `shell_targets()` walk ALREADY parsed that same `mkdir -p` as a `mkdir_p` target moments earlier — the information needed to resolve the `cd` was in hand and simply never fed back into the existence model. Note the failure is an ASK, not a DENY (`:607` states the never-deny-a-guessed-path policy deliberately), so it is milder than [[ORCH-058]]'s denial — but under LSN-036 an ASK on a legitimate read-only operation still costs a human keypress and still trains bypass, which is the damage the lesson is actually about. Not fixed here, and deliberately NOT patched locally: the standing rule is that quality-gated-dev-workflow package bugs ship UPSTREAM, because a local patch is a fork the next reinstall silently reverts — proven both ways on 2026-08-05. Suggested upstream fix, stated so it is reviewable rather than hand-waved: while walking the command left to right, treat a successfully-parsed `mkdir`/`mkdir -p` of an ABSOLUTE path as making that directory exist for the remainder of the same command's resolution, so a subsequent `cd` into it resolves. That is strictly narrower than relaxing `_resolve_cd`, and it cannot introduce a clobber blind spot, because the `mkdir` target itself is still classified by the existing rules | Sibling in kind to [[ORCH-058]] and [[ORCH-062]] — same hook, third and now fourth distinct detection path (snapshot-restore suffix regex; new-file-creation regex; and now `cd`-resolution existence checking). Workaround in use meanwhile, and it is a workaround for the AGENT, not for the guard: write scratch scripts with the Write tool and invoke them by absolute path, or run the `mkdir` as its own separate step, rather than chaining. [[LSN-036]] miss recorded (miss:5) in the same edit as this row. No REQ allocated; flagged for whoever next owns hook maintenance upstream. |
| ORCH-064 | Found by `garmin_inspector_v1_30` 2026-09-19 after the USER asked why no wake had fired even though the reviewer had finished 14 minutes earlier — the Inspector had reported the wake "armed successfully" on the absence of an immediate error rather than on evidence it could fire | non-blocking — Inspector tooling, no product code affected | insp_wake.sh never fired on its success path | **FIXED 2026-09-19.** `exec > >(tee -a $WAKELOG)` makes `tee` a child of the script; the bare `wait` after `kill $(jobs -p)` waits for every child including `tee`, which cannot exit while the script still holds the pipe open. Deadlock. Reproduced standalone (exit 124, the block never printed) and re-verified after the fix (exit 0; the real script now prints the block and exits). Fix: wait on the `herdr agent wait` children BY PID, never via bare `wait`/`jobs -p`. Why it stayed invisible: the roster-error path `exit 1`s before reaching any `wait`, so the only arm that had ever produced output was a failure, and the success path had never once completed. BLOCKS: {} | — |
| ORCH-065 | Found by `garmin_inspector_v1_30` 2026-09-19 while fixing [[ORCH-064]], not reported separately by anyone | non-blocking — Inspector tooling | Wake counted the Inspector's own pane as a supervised worker | **FIXED 2026-09-19.** `WORKING` was built from every agent in `herdr agent list`, and the Inspector's own pane is always `working` while it runs — so the all-settled branch was unreachable, and the wake armed `--until idle` against the Inspector itself, a condition satisfied the moment it ends a turn. Worth keeping because of the interaction: fixing [[ORCH-064]] alone would have replaced silence with a wake firing on every turn-end instead of on a supervised agent. Fix: restrict `WORKING` to the three roster panes. BLOCKS: {} | — |
| ORCH-066 | Found by `garmin_inspector_v1_30` 2026-09-19 when three consecutive `collect` calls returned `truncated` for a 16-line reply that could not have scrolled; the Inspector read the pane itself instead of trusting the classification | non-blocking — Inspector tooling, but it DISCARDS REAL WORK | dispatch.py classified every Codex reply as truncated | **FIXED 2026-09-19.** Codex's TUI renders a reply as a bulleted list, so the opening sentinel arrives as `- <<<BEGIN unit>>>` while the closing one often renders bare; `marker_line_re`'s whole-line anchor matched only END and the classifier took its ends-without-begins branch. MEASURED on the live pane: BEGIN whole-line matches 0, loose matches 2, END whole-line 1. Fix: an optional list-marker prefix, with the trailing `$` retained so the echoed DELIVER sentence still cannot match (verified 0 matches). Live `collect` then returned `complete`. THE COST IS HISTORICAL, NOT HYPOTHETICAL: this is the likely mechanism behind the [[B-STAGE9-25]] round-3 reviewer that "sat blocked without ever returning a verdict" — the verdict existed and the tooling threw it away, so a repair round was carried forward as unreviewed. | ORCH-067 | Raised by the USER 2026-09-19, twice: first "why you did not receive any wake so far", then "why did you decide to stop?" Both times the Inspector (`garmin_inspector_v1_30`) had reported the loop healthy. Filed because the second question exposed a defect the first one's fix did not touch | non-blocking — process/skill defect, no product code affected | Arming a wake with nothing dispatched is a no-op that reads as supervision | **open.** The Inspector closed an atomic unit, named the next one (B-STAGE9-12), did NOT dispatch it, armed `insp_wake.sh` and ended the turn. With all three agents idle the wake takes its settled branch and waits `--until working` — a transition only a dispatch from the Inspector can cause — so the loop could not self-advance and the next wake was a pure poll. The `SKILL.md` invariant that was satisfied is the MECHANICAL one ("never end a turn without a wake armed"); the actual requirement (keep going) is checked by nothing, so arming the wake functioned as a substitute for working. Two aggravating factors, both the Inspector's: it cited its own context at 176k against a 210k threshold as if succession were imminent, and it treated a landed commit as a finish line. PARTIAL FIX APPLIED 2026-09-19 (uncommitted at time of writing): the settled heartbeat cut 900000 -> 300000 so a stall surfaces in 5 minutes rather than 15, the header now warns that passing both positional args overrides the defaults (editing the default alone silently does nothing), and `SKILL.md` gained an explicit clause that all-idle-with-nothing-dispatched is always an Inspector error, not a wait. WHAT IS OWED: the heartbeat change treats VISIBILITY only — no timeout length can release a branch that waits on a dispatch nobody made. A mechanical guard belongs in the wake itself: refuse to arm, or print a loud stall warning, when every roster pane is settled and no work is outstanding. BLOCKS: {} | — |

| B-STAGE9-05 | Reviewer delta-check/DEC-052 fix round (2026-09-13) | blocking | The builder's B-STAGE9-04 fix (`if (PyGILState_Check()) PyEval_SaveThread();` in `PyProcessBootstrap.cpp`'s already-initialized-externally branch) was itself unsafe: `PyGILState_Check()` only reports whether the CALLING thread currently holds the GIL — it cannot distinguish "held incidentally because whatever externally initialized Python also left it held" from "held deliberately by the calling thread's own unrelated `PyGILState_Ensure()` scope for completely different work." On the first-ever process-wide call to `ensureInitialized()` (the only call that can reach this branch, per the `g_attempted` guard), if the calling thread happened to already hold the GIL for its own reasons, this code would silently steal it via `PyEval_SaveThread()`, corrupting the interpreter's per-thread GIL state out from under the legitimate owner. Confirmed as real, not theoretical: a new regression test (a re-exec'd child process that externally initializes Python, acquires the GIL for unrelated reasons, then makes the process's first `ensureInitialized()` call) triggered a fatal Python abort (`PyGILState_Release: thread state must be current when releasing`) against the buggy code, proving genuine undefined behavior, not just a documentation mismatch | **FIXED (2026-09-13).** The `PyGILState_Check()`/`PyEval_SaveThread()` call is removed entirely from the already-initialized-externally branch — it now touches no GIL state in either direction, leaving the calling thread's state exactly as found. `PyProcessBootstrap.h`'s and `PythonEmbed.cpp`'s documented/commented contracts narrowed to match: the "GIL released on return" guarantee applies only to the path where the call performed the real `Py_InitializeFromConfig()`; the externally-initialized path leaves GIL state untouched/unknown, and callers must always use their own `PyGILState_Ensure()`/`Release()` (safe either way) rather than assume a particular state. | B-STAGE9-06 | Stage 9 live-account re-test attempt #2 (2026-09-13, post-DEC-052 fix) | blocking | With B-STAGE9-01's code defect fixed and committed (`173135907`), the user re-attempted a real Garmin login through the qmake release binary (`GC_WANT_GARMINCONNECT=ON`/`GC_WANT_PYTHON` OFF). Python now genuinely bootstraps and runs (proving B-STAGE9-01's fix works) and the UI correctly showed the classified `GarminErrors::translate(Network)` message ("Couldn't reach Garmin Connect...") instead of the old generic `Unknown` fold — but the underlying `garminconnect` 0.3.15 auth-strategy chain failed end-to-end (`~/.goldencheetah/goldencheetah.log`): `mobile+cffi` and `widget+cffi`/`portal+cffi` all raised `ImpersonateError: Impersonating chrome120/chrome150 is not supported`; `mobile+requests` returned a real `429` (`GarminConnectTooManyRequestsError`, IP rate-limited by Garmin); `portal+requests` hit a real `403` (Cloudflare bot challenge) on one attempt and a genuine `AttributeError: 'RequestsCookieJar' object has no attribute 'jar'` on another (a real code-path bug inside the `garminconnect`/`curl_cffi` fallback, not environmental). The Inspector independently reproduced the EXACT SAME `curl_cffi.requests.Session(impersonate="chrome120"/"chrome150")` calls from a plain `python3 -c "..."` CLI process (same user, same site-packages, same curl_cffi 0.16.3) and they succeeded with real HTTP 200s — ruling out a missing/wrong package version. Also ruled out: a second shadowing `curl_cffi` install anywhere on the machine (only one exists). Initially also suspected NOT to be a symbol collision with GoldenCheetah's own linked `libcurl-gnutls.so.4` because `curl_cffi/_wrapper.abi3.so` has zero *dynamic* `libcurl*` NEEDED-entry dependency per `ldd` — **this inference was WRONG, corrected by the investigator's repro below**: a statically-linked library's own exported (non-`-Bsymbolic`) symbols are still preemptible via the dynamic linker's normal global symbol resolution even with no `NEEDED` entry, if a same-named symbol is already loaded globally elsewhere in the process | **FIXED (2026-09-13, builder GREEN + reviewer delta-check PASS). COMMITTED `0c05f7c18`.** `pch_investigator`'s controlled A/B reproduction (scratch-dir harness, `/tmp/pch_investigator/`, no GoldenCheetah files touched): a bare embedded-CPython process (no Qt) does NOT reproduce the failure on either the init thread or an explicit pthread worker — ruling out CPython embedding and thread affinity entirely. Globally `dlopen`ing `libcurl-gnutls.so.4` (GoldenCheetah's own real linked dependency, confirmed via `ldd`) BEFORE importing `curl_cffi` in that same bare harness DOES reproduce the exact `ImpersonateError` — confirmed via `readelf`/`nm` that `curl_easy_init`/`curl_version` then bind to the system GnuTLS libcurl while `curl_easy_impersonate` (a symbol that only exists in curl_cffi's own bundled, patched, BoringSSL-based libcurl-impersonate) still binds to curl_cffi's own `.so` — a genuine ABI/internal-state mismatch (a `CURL*` built by the wrong `curl_easy_init` handed to the real `curl_easy_impersonate`, which then sees the wrong `curl_version` string and concludes the Chrome target isn't supported). Importing curl_cffi's wrapper with Python's `os.RTLD_DEEPBIND` dlopen flag (confirmed in the same repro) forces it to bind to itself and restores both `chrome120`/`chrome150`. Root cause: ELF symbol interposition, not CPython/GIL/Qt-threading. Fix dispatched to the builder: apply the `RTLD_DEEPBIND` guard in `src/Python/garminconnect/garmin_client.py` (the single documented choke point that imports `garminconnect`/`curl_cffi`), guarded by `hasattr(os, "RTLD_DEEPBIND")` for macOS/Windows portability, with a regression test that deliberately recreates the interposition precondition (RED first) before asserting the fix (GREEN). Separately, the investigator flagged a non-causal, pre-existing dangling-pointer bug (`PythonEmbed.cpp` `Py_SetProgramName()` on a temporary's buffer) — tracked as B-STAGE9-07, queued for the builder next. Builder applied the `RTLD_DEEPBIND` guard exactly as dispatched (`src/Python/garminconnect/garmin_client.py`), added a regression test (`src/Python/garminconnect/tests/test_curl_cffi_interposition.py`, subprocess-based, deliberately recreates the interposition precondition) and its `garmin-py`-labelled ctest wiring; manual RED (rc=48) / GREEN (rc=0) cycle confirmed against the real installed curl_cffi 0.16.3, full suite `ctest -L "garmin-fast\|garmin-py"` 43/43 (was 42, +1 new). `garmin_codex_reviewer` independently delta-checked the diff (dlopen-flags save/restore under exception, thread-safety, subprocess-repro fidelity, scope) — PASS, no defects. | B-STAGE9-07 | pch_investigator B-STAGE9-06 root-cause pass, non-causal side finding (2026-09-13) | non-blocking | `src/Python/PythonEmbed.cpp` (~line 264) calls `Py_SetProgramName((wchar_t*) pybin.toStdWString().c_str())` — the `std::wstring` returned by `toStdWString()` is a temporary; its buffer is destroyed at the end of the full expression, but CPython's own documentation requires the pointer passed to `Py_SetProgramName()` to remain valid for the entire process lifetime. **Builder-corrected nuance:** on Python 3.13 (this build) `Py_SetProgramName()` actually copies its argument internally, so the dangling pointer is not observable UB here; the borrowed-pointer contract (and therefore the real bug) applies to Python <= 3.12, which GoldenCheetah also ships against (3.11, per `e0a198a16`; the Linux deployment path hardcodes `/opt/python3.11`) — still a genuine, worth-fixing contract violation across the project's supported Python range, not a live UAF on every build. Pre-existing code, NOT introduced by this session's DEC-052 diff — traced back through several historical commits (`e0a198a16` "Fix python embedding crash with Python 3.11", `1dc00b8bd` "Setenv PYTHONHOME not Py_SetPythonHome", predating both). Confirmed non-causal for B-STAGE9-06 (the curl_cffi ELF-interposition bug) — found only as a side observation while reading this file for that investigation | **FIXED (2026-09-13, builder GREEN + reviewer delta-check PASS). COMMITTED `28958dc16`.** User decision recorded 2026-09-13 (`AskUserQuestion`, fix-now vs defer per this project's pre-existing/foundational-code scope-boundary policy): **fix now**, as its own atomic unit, separate commit from B-STAGE9-06. New `PyProcessBootstrap::setProgramName(QString)` (`src/Python/PyProcessBootstrap.{h,cpp}`) retains every handed-over name in a function-local `static std::list<std::wstring>` (list nodes never move, entries never freed), so a later call (e.g. a second `PythonEmbed` from `main.cpp`'s in-process restart loop) can't dangle a pointer CPython already holds from an earlier call. Placed in `PyProcessBootstrap` rather than `PythonEmbed.cpp` because the latter cannot be linked into any unittest target (needs the appsettings/Gui chain), while the former already compiles into test targets. `PythonEmbed.cpp`'s one call site now delegates to it (comment-only otherwise, out of clang-format's DEC-010 scope). New regression `unittests/Core/garminconnect/testPythonProgramNameLifetime.cpp` interposes its own `Py_SetProgramName` at link order (same ELF mechanism B-STAGE9-06 documented) modeling the <=3.12 borrowed-pointer contract under ASan (build FATAL_ERRORs without sanitizer support, matching `testGarminConnectSyncDialogClose` precedent); 2 slots (single-call lifetime, repeated-call restart-safety). RED = ASan heap-use-after-free against the buggy pattern verbatim; GREEN = 4/4 direct, full `ctest -L "garmin-fast\|garmin-py"` 44/44 (was 43, +1 new). `garmin_codex_reviewer` independently delta-checked (thread-safety of the static list — only reachable from `main.cpp`'s single main-thread call site, confirmed; link-seam interposition order verified against generated Ninja rules; scope; ASan-gate precedent) — PASS, no code defect (flagged only that the 2 new evidence-seal `.log` files sit outside the reviewed path list, an established untracked-by-design artifact per this project's existing uncommitted seal logs) | User `AskUserQuestion` decision, 2026-09-13, prompted by `pch_investigator`'s B-STAGE9-06 investigation; fix built + independently reviewer-verified 2026-09-13, committed `28958dc16`; Inspector independently rebuilt (full `GoldenCheetah` app target + all unittests, exit 0) and re-ran the full suite post-commit — 44/44 PASS, 0 failures |
| B-STAGE9-08 | Stage 9 diagnostic-logging enhancement (not its own ledgered REQ), reviewer delta-check chain, 8 rounds (2026-09-13) | blocking | New Stage-9 diagnostic feature: `PyAuthOutcome`/`GarminAuthFailure` gain an `exceptionType` field (a module-qualified Python exception TYPE name only, e.g. `builtins.ValueError` -- deliberately never message/argument content) via new `src/Cloud/PyEmbeddedAdapter.cpp` helper `pyExceptionTypeName()`, logged via a new `qDebug` line in `GarminCredentialsPage.cpp`/`GarminMfaPage.cpp` on the `Unknown` auth-failure branch only, to help triage further live-account retest failures. Across 8 successive reviewer delta-check rounds, each closed a DISTINCT real bypass letting an adversarial/foreign Python exception object smuggle untrusted string content into this logged field: (1) a `toQString()`/`str(o)` fallback could stringify a non-`str` `__module__`/`__qualname__`, running arbitrary `__str__`; (2) the `tp_name` fallback double-prepended an already-qualified module prefix; (3) neither `__module__` nor `__qualname__` nor `tp_name` was checked for actually being identifier-shaped (grammar), letting an identifier-shaped secret (e.g. `sk_live_abc123`) pass through verbatim; (4) even a grammar-valid module string could BE a secret, closed by a fixed `kAllowedModuleRoots[]` allowlist (`builtins`, `garmin_client`, `garminconnect`, `curl_cffi`, `requests`, `urllib3`, `socket`, `ssl`, `http`, `json` -- this integration's actual, empirically-verified dependency chain) checked via `isAllowedModuleRoot()`; (5) the allowlist check only fired when `moduleName` was non-empty, so an absent/invalid `__module__` bypassed it entirely via the `tp_name` fallback or a `"builtins."+qualName` default; (6) even with (5) unified into one gate, `__module__`/`__qualname__` are ordinary writable attributes ANY class can claim without compromising the real dependency -- closed by `isGenuineAllowlistedException()`, which resolves the LONGEST real `sys.modules` prefix of the claimed dotted path, walks the remainder as a real attribute chain, and compares the result to the actual exception type by RAW POINTER IDENTITY (never a Python-level `==`, which a foreign class could override); (7) the `tp_name`-fallback branch's provenance check proved the resolved object was REAL but never that its OWN module root was on the allowlist -- a foreign type could claim an allowlisted `__module__` (passing gate 4/5), suppress `__qualname__` (forcing the `tp_name` path), and have a genuinely-real `tp_name` from an unallowlisted, unvetted module reachable in-process. Rounds 6-8's design/scoping calls (identity-verification approach; the round-8 choice of an independent allowlist check on `tp_name`'s own module portion rather than exact equality with `moduleName`, which would have wrongly broken the legitimate round-2 `TpNameFallbackError` regression) were Inspector-made per the autonomy-boundary GO list (technical decisions within this fix's own new-code scope), not user escalations | **FIXED (2026-09-13), all 8 rounds, reviewer GREEN on round 8's delta-check.** Final invariant: `pyExceptionTypeName()` emits a derived name only when its claimed module is {present, Unicode, grammar-valid, allowlisted} AND (for the `tp_name` path) that path's own module root is independently allowlisted AND the resolved type object is proven, by real `sys.modules` lookup + raw pointer identity (not any attacker-overridable comparison), to actually BE the object it claims to be -- anything failing any leg folds to the fixed marker `"foreign_exception"`. New regression coverage in `testGarminConnectPyAdapter.cpp`/`pystubs/garmin_client.py` for every round's bypass, including two genuinely dependency-backed positive-path tests (`genuineDependencyBackedExceptionStillProducesFullyQualifiedName` imports the real installed `requests` package and raises its actual `ConnectionError`; `TpNameNotAllowlistedError` is genuinely attached as a real attribute of the real imported `os` module to prove the round-8 check, not just a fabricated string, is what rejects it) rather than only synthetic/unbacked fixtures. Final counts: `testGarminConnectPyAdapter` 49/49; full rebuild exit 0; `ctest -L garmin-fast` 40/40; `ctest -L garmin-py` 4/4 -- Inspector independently rebuilt (`ninja`: no work to do, already current) and re-ran both labels directly post-round-8, matching the builder's and reviewer's reports exactly (garmin-fast 317.23s real, 0 failures). Also fixed en route (rounds 1-2, folded into this same chain rather than split out): a duplicated-per-test-file `qDebug`-capture helper was replaced with one shared, mutex-protected `unittests/Core/garminconnect/MessageCapture.h` mirroring the already-reviewed `ObsCapture` shape (B-OBS001-02 precedent) | 8 reviewer delta-check rounds (`garmin_codex_reviewer`, gpt-5.6-terra high) on the real diff, dispatched by the Inspector 2026-09-13, each finding traced to file:line before the next fix round; Inspector read every diff directly before each dispatch (not just relaying builder self-reports); Inspector independently rebuilt + re-ran the full `garmin-fast`+`garmin-py` suite post-round-8 (exit 0, 44/44 combined, 0 failures) before this closure was recorded |
| B-STAGE9-09 | Stage 9 live-account connect re-test attempt #4 (2026-09-13), root-caused by B-STAGE9-08's own new diagnostic | blocking | With B-STAGE9-01..08 all fixed and committed, the user re-attempted a real Garmin login through the qmake release binary (`GC_WANT_GARMINCONNECT=ON`/`GC_WANT_PYTHON` OFF) four times; every attempt showed the generic `GarminErrors::translate(Unknown)` fold ("Connection to Garmin Connect failed (code: unknown)"). `~/.goldencheetah/goldencheetah.log` showed, per attempt, only two warnings — `mobile+cffi returned 429` and `mobile+requests returned 429` (`GarminConnectTooManyRequestsError`, IP rate-limited by Garmin) — initially read as "the 5-strategy chain stops after 2/5 AND mis-classifies an all-429 outcome as Unknown instead of `rate_limit`". **Both halves of that initial reading were wrong.** B-STAGE9-08's new `exceptionType` diagnostic — its first live use — answered it directly: log lines 14-17 carry `Debug: garmin_auth_unknown exception_type=builtins.AttributeError`, one per attempt. (These lines were at first reported as ABSENT from the log, which seeded a false second question about whether `GarminCredentialsPage.cpp:145`'s `qDebug` was reachable at all; they are present, just further down the file after the Wayland-plugin line rather than adjacent to the 429 pairs — an incomplete/stale log read, not a wiring gap. `myMessageOutput`'s `GC_WANT_HTTP` install site is irrelevant here: Qt's default handler already writes `qDebug` to stderr, which `main.cpp` redirects into `goldencheetah.log`.) Real root cause: **the login SUCCEEDS.** Strategies 1-2 (`mobile+cffi`, `mobile+requests`) are 429-rate-limited and log at WARNING; strategy 3 (`widget+cffi`, a plain HTML SSO flow over curl_cffi with no Qt/WebEngine dependency despite the "widget" name) then authenticates successfully and `login()` returns early by design — which is why strategies 4-5 never appear and why only 2 lines are ever logged. The failure is POST-login, in OUR OWN adapter: `src/Python/garminconnect/garmin_client.py:137` (`_login_impl`) and `:242` (`_submit_mfa_impl`) both build the identity dict with `str(self._garmin.full_name_id)`, but the real installed `python-garminconnect` 0.3.15 `Garmin` object HAS NO `full_name_id` attribute (it exposes `display_name` — Garmin's socialProfile `displayName`, the stable profile GUID the library itself uses to build every API URL, and filename-safe — plus `full_name`, a human name that is not; the raw profile dict carrying a numeric `profileId` is discarded by the library and unreachable without an extra network call). Second, compounding defect: that identity-dict construction sits OUTSIDE `_login_impl`'s `try/except` classification block, so the resulting `AttributeError` escapes as a raw foreign `builtins.*` exception, `PyEmbeddedAdapter::classifyPendingException()` correctly folds it to `GarminAuthFailure::Unknown`, and the UI renders the useless generic copy — i.e. ANY library-shape mismatch, not just this one, is undiagnosable from the UI by construction. Why the test suite never caught it: the fakes in `tests/test_adapter_login.py`/`test_adapter_mfa.py` DEFINE `full_name_id`, so the fake agreed with the bug — and A3-REQ-002's mutation testing (M8/M9, `archive/a3-req-002.md`) recorded both `full_name_id`↔`display_name` swap mutants as KILLED by the happy-path identity assertion, i.e. the mutation score actively certified the defect. This is `DEC-014` OQ1 ("real-library signature unconfirmed against the not-yet-bundled wheel; pinned by the fakes/pystub") coming due exactly as written | **FIXED (2026-09-13), builder GREEN + `garmin_codex_reviewer` delta-check PASS. COMMITTED `4cffe2835`. CONFIRMED ON THE LIVE ACCOUNT (re-test attempt #5, same day): the user completed the credentials page, accepted the Garmin-restriction dialogs, and Garmin Connect now appears as a configured account in Athlete settings > Accounts — the first time Stage 9's connect has ever succeeded. Live evidence: `~/.goldencheetah/goldencheetah.log` (truncated on relaunch, 6 lines) carries the two harmless `mobile+cffi`/`mobile+requests` 429 warnings and **NO `garmin_auth_unknown exception_type=` line at all** — the generic Unknown fold is gone; `~/.goldencheetah/Andy/config/garminconnect/active-account.json` was written 0600 with `garmin_user_id` = a `ee9c52d8…` GUID, confirming the Inspector's `display_name` choice resolves to Garmin's stable, filename-safe socialProfile profile GUID exactly as predicted. Verification counts (garmin-fast 40/40 and garmin-py 5/5 both independently re-run by the Inspector, not taken from the builder's report; pytest 54/54; ruff/ruff-format/mypy --strict/ledger-drift all PASS at the pre-commit gate).** Dispatched as ONE atomic unit, TDD RED-first, three parts: (1) correctness — `garmin_user_id` reads `self._garmin.display_name` at BOTH call sites (`:137`, `:242`); Inspector-made technical decision per the autonomy-boundary GO list (no extra network call; `design.md:221` already sanctions `display_name` as the id source), the resulting duplication with the `"display_name"` key is intentional; (2) robustness — bring identity extraction under exception classification in both `_login_impl` and `_submit_mfa_impl` so a library-shape mismatch raises a `GarminError` with a real kind instead of escaping raw, without weakening REQ-005 (no password retention) or REQ-014/LSN-006 (classify by exception TYPE, never message text); (3) a fake-fidelity/contract test asserting every library attribute the adapter reads actually exists on the REAL installed `garminconnect.Garmin`, skipped cleanly when the wheel is absent so wheel-less CI stays green, plus fake updates to mirror the real surface. Cascade owned by the Inspector, not the builder: `design.md:221`'s prose is also wrong ("we use the numeric ID for filename safety" — no numeric id is exposed by this library), `DEC-014` OQ1 is now answerable from real evidence and should be closed, and a lessons entry is owed on fakes pinning a non-existent library API (the failure mode that let mutation testing certify the bug). BLOCKS: {STAGE:9, TASK:REQ-002, TASK:REQ-003} — pending builder GREEN, `garmin_codex_reviewer` delta-check, qmake rebuild, and Stage 9 live-account re-test attempt #5 | Live Stage-9 manual connect re-test (user-driven, real credentials, real Garmin account) 2026-09-13, attempt #4; root cause established by the Inspector directly from `~/.goldencheetah/goldencheetah.log` lines 14-17 plus an attribute probe against the installed wheel (`hasattr(gc.Garmin(...), 'full_name_id')` → False), and INDEPENDENTLY confirmed by `pch_investigator` (gpt-5.6-terra high, isolated `/tmp` scratch dir, read-only on project files, no real credentials), whose no-network controlled probe of the actual adapter reproduced the exact `builtins.AttributeError: 'Garmin' object has no attribute 'full_name_id'` and which separately verified by controlled injection that all five strategies DO run when none succeeds (2×429 + 3 ordinary → final connection error; 5×429 → final `GarminConnectTooManyRequestsError`), i.e. the "loop stops at 2/5" reading was an artifact of strategy 3 succeeding, and that only a `BaseException` such as `SystemExit` can bypass the per-strategy handlers |
| B-STAGE9-10 | Inspector real-library contract audit of `garmin_client.py`, prompted by B-STAGE9-09 (2026-09-13) | blocking | Auditing the REST of the adapter's real-library contract after B-STAGE9-09 (same root cause: DEC-014 OQ1 surfaces pinned by fakes, never checked against the installed wheel) found REQ-003's ENTIRE two-step MFA seam is non-functional against python-garminconnect 0.3.15 — three compounding defects, none reachable by the current fake-based suite. (1) **The needs-MFA sentinel can never be produced.** `garmin_client.py:104` constructs `_gc.Garmin(email, password)` with defaults, i.e. `return_on_mfa=False` and `prompt_mfa=None` (verified: `inspect.signature(gc.Garmin.__init__)`). With both unset, `client.login()`'s `resolve_mfa()` (client.py:532) takes neither the `return_on_mfa` branch (which returns the `("needs_mfa", None)` tuple) nor the `prompt_mfa` branch, and falls through to `raise GarminConnectAuthenticationError("MFA Required but no prompt_mfa mechanism supplied")` (client.py:547). So `_login_impl`'s shape check at `:133` (`result[0] == "needs_mfa"`) can never match; an MFA-enabled account gets `GarminError(kind='auth')` and the Slice-B page renders generic bad-credentials copy instead of ever prompting for the OTP. (2) **`resume_login`'s arguments are reversed, and the OTP is discarded.** Real signature is `resume_login(client_state, mfa_code)` (wrapper `__init__.py`; underlying `client.py:1620` is `resume_login(_client_state, mfa_code)`). `garmin_client.py:230` calls `self._garmin.resume_login(code, pending)` — so the user's OTP is passed as the IGNORED `_client_state` parameter (underscore-prefixed and genuinely unused in the body) and the pending-state object is submitted to `_complete_mfa()` AS the verification code. Every OTP submission therefore fails by construction, and — because `resume_login` leaves the pending session intact on a failed code by design — it fails identically on all three REQ-003 retries. (3) **The client_state carry model does not exist in this library.** `resolve_mfa` returns `("needs_mfa", None)`: there is no opaque state blob to carry, the pending session is held on the `Garmin` instance itself (`client._mfa_pending`, cleared by `_clear_mfa_pending_state()`). `_login_impl:134` would store `self._pending_mfa = None`, and `_submit_mfa_impl:221`'s `if pending is None` guard would then reject the user's own valid OTP with the contract-error message "submit_mfa() called with no pending MFA session; call login() first". The adapter already holds one long-lived `self._garmin`, so instance-held state is workable — but the plumbing must change to a boolean/flag model, not an opaque-blob model. Note the non-obvious trap for the fix: simply setting `return_on_mfa=True` at construction is NOT sufficient and regresses the happy path — the wrapper's `login()` returns EARLY in that mode (`__init__.py:738-743`) and skips `_load_profile_and_settings()`, so `display_name` (B-STAGE9-09's newly-chosen `garmin_user_id` source) would be unpopulated on every NON-MFA login. `resume_login()` does call `_load_profile_and_settings()` itself, so only the non-MFA branch needs the compensating load | **FIXED (2026-09-13), builder GREEN + `garmin_codex_reviewer` delta-check round 1 FAIL -> repair -> round 2 PASS (no findings). COMMITTED `e5936375c`.** Implemented as designed: `_gc.Garmin(email, password, return_on_mfa=True)` unconditionally; `self._pending_mfa` is now a plain boolean sentinel rather than a carried payload (the real `resume_login` never reads its first parameter, so there is nothing meaningful to carry); `self._garmin.resume_login(None, code)` with the OTP in the SECOND positional slot. The non-obvious trap documented in this row was compensated exactly as predicted: `_login_impl` now calls `self._garmin._load_profile_and_settings()` explicitly on the non-MFA success path, because `return_on_mfa=True` makes the wrapper's `login()` return early UNCONDITIONALLY (including on plain non-MFA success) and skip its own profile load. The `resume_login` success path deliberately does NOT repeat that call — the library's own wrapper already makes it. **Reviewer round 1 returned three BLOCKING findings, ALL independently confirmed by the Inspector against the real wheel before being sent back, and all the same class as [[B-STAGE9-09]] — an exception raised OUTSIDE a classification try/except, escaping raw and folding to the generic "code: unknown":** (1) the new `_load_profile_and_settings()` call was itself unclassified — i.e. THE FIX FOR THIS FINDING RE-CREATED B-STAGE9-09's BUG PATTERN ON A NEW LINE, caught only because the delta-check runs before commit; (2) [[B-STAGE9-11]]'s `load_tokens` half (see that row); (3) `_submit_mfa_impl` left a POST-OTP `GarminConnectConnectionError` ("token rejected by API tier after MFA", raised after a CORRECT code is accepted) and the rate-limit case unclassified, bypassing the transient-retry policy entirely. All three repaired RED-first; reviewer round 2 returned no findings. Verification counts INDEPENDENTLY re-run by the Inspector rather than relayed: pytest 64/64, mypy --strict clean (16 files), ruff clean, real-wheel contract test confirmed EXECUTING not skipping, and all eight fix points mutation-tested — reverting each individually makes the suite fail, proving the rewritten fakes did not weaken coverage (the specific risk, since fakes across 7 test files were rewritten and `test_adapter_mfa.py`'s had encoded the OLD reversed argument order as real assertions). Residual accepted as NON-BLOCKING: `resume_login`'s first argument is passed as `None`; correct against 0.3.15 (the inner parameter is `_client_state` and is provably never read in the body) and now pinned by a contract assertion that the inner first parameter still starts with `_`, so a future wheel that begins reading it fails loudly. — original assessment retained below — **OPEN, blocking for Stage 9's MFA acceptance criterion — NOT blocking live re-test attempt #5.** Deliberately NOT bundled into B-STAGE9-09's atomic unit: (a) the user's live account is demonstrably not MFA-gated (attempts #3/#4 reached the POST-login identity read with no OTP challenge, so login completed unchallenged), hence this cannot affect attempt #5; (b) unlike B-STAGE9-09's one-line attribute correction this needs a real design decision — how to bridge the project's opaque-client_state MFA model to the library's instance-held one without regressing the non-MFA profile load — plus its own reviewer pass; (c) bundling would delay a live retest already in flight. Queued as the NEXT atomic unit for `garmin_builder_stage9_v3` after B-STAGE9-09 lands, to be dispatched with the same fake-fidelity discipline (a real-wheel contract test over `Garmin.__init__` kwargs and the `resume_login` parameter ORDER, skipped-not-failed when the wheel is absent — a signature test, since an arg-order defect is invisible to a `hasattr` check). BLOCKS: {STAGE:9, TASK:REQ-003} | Established by the Inspector 2026-09-13 by direct introspection of the installed wheel (`inspect.signature(gc.Garmin.__init__)`, `inspect.signature(gc.Garmin.resume_login)`, `inspect.getsource(gc.Garmin.resume_login)`) and by reading `client.py:528-548` (`resolve_mfa`) and `client.py:1620-1633` (`client.resume_login`, whose first parameter is `_client_state` and is never referenced in the body) against `garmin_client.py:104`, `:133-134`, `:218-231`. Not yet independently re-verified by reviewer or investigator — that is owed at dispatch time, per the standing rule that an Inspector's own read is not a substitute for an independent one |
| B-STAGE9-11 | Builder out-of-scope flag during B-STAGE9-09 (2026-09-13), independently re-verified by the Inspector | blocking | Third instance of B-STAGE9-09's defect class (a fake pinning a library surface that was never checked against the real wheel — DEC-014 OQ1's remaining half, the one explicitly left open when OQ1's identity-attribute half was closed earlier the same day). REQ-004 token persistence calls `self._garmin.dumps()` (`garmin_client.py:188`, `dump_tokens()`) and the matching `loads()` (`load_tokens()`, `:213-218`). The real installed python-garminconnect 0.3.15 `Garmin` object exposes NEITHER — verified by direct introspection: `hasattr(g,'dumps')`, `hasattr(g,'loads')`, `hasattr(g,'dump')`, `hasattr(g,'load')`, `hasattr(g,'garth')` are ALL False. **Correction to the builder's report, which called token export/import "likely completely broken":** the methods do exist, one level in — `g.client.dumps` and `g.client.loads` are both present on the INNER client object (the same `self.client` the wrapper's own `login()` uses for `load()`/`dump()`). The remedy is therefore a one-level indirection (`self._garmin.client.dumps()` / `.loads()`), not a redesign, and the DEC-014 Option B contract (library constructed AUTH-ONLY with no tokenstore path; C++ `GarminTokenStore` owns the single atomic 0600 write of the exported blob) is unaffected — only the accessor path changes. Effect on a live account: `dumps()` raises `AttributeError`, so `dump_tokens()` fails, so the connect flow persists an EMPTY token blob and the session is not restorable across restarts (REQ-004 unmet); `load_tokens()` would likewise fail on any restore path | **FIXED (2026-09-13), COMMITTED `e5936375c`, reviewer round 2 PASS.** `dump_tokens()` now calls `self._garmin.client.dumps()` and `load_tokens()` `self._garmin.client.loads(token_str)` — the one-level indirection this row predicted; no fallback getattr chain, deliberately, so a future real break fails loudly via the contract test rather than being silently absorbed. **The reviewer's round-1 delta-check found a SECOND, previously unrecorded half of this defect, confirmed empirically by the Inspector against the installed wheel:** `load_tokens` caught only `GarminConnectAuthenticationError`, but the real inner `Client.loads()` wraps EVERY structural failure of the blob — empty string, malformed JSON, `{}`, and a well-formed-but-wrong blob alike — into `GarminConnectConnectionError`. So no malformed or empty blob ever reached the `session_expired` kind that DEC-014 OQ2 / REQ-NF-Compat-001(b) promise; they all escaped raw and folded to Unknown. This directly explains the live evidence: the four post-exit `gc_obs op=auth outcome=fail error_code=unknown` traces (see [[B-STAGE9-12]]) read `unknown` rather than `session_expired` precisely because the user's 0-byte `tokens.json` took this uncaught path. The handler now catches both exception types and maps them specifically to `session_expired`. Note the fake that hid it: `tests/test_token_store.py` modelled malformed input as an AUTHENTICATION exception — a fake agreeing with the adapter's assumption instead of the library's real behaviour, which is DEC-014 OQ1's failure mode a third time over; the fake was corrected to raise what the real library raises. Still OPEN as a separate question, deliberately NOT bundled here: whether `GarminTokenStore::loadChecked()` accepting a present 0-byte file as `LoadStatus::Ok` (`src/Cloud/GarminTokenStore.cpp`) warrants a C++ defence-in-depth guard, since the Python fix alone leaves that acceptance unchanged. That is a C++ change needing its own unit and its own reviewer pass. Verification INDEPENDENTLY re-run by the Inspector: pytest 64/64, mypy --strict clean, ruff clean, contract test EXECUTING, and both `.client.dumps`/`.client.loads` fix points plus the new `session_expired` classification mutation-tested. — prior escalation and original assessment retained below — **SEVERITY ESCALATED 2026-09-13 (evening), live-confirmed: this is a HARD BLOCKER ON THE ENTIRE SYNC/DOWNLOAD PATH, not merely a re-enter-credentials-after-restart annoyance as originally assessed below.** The original assessment was correct that it cannot fail the CONNECT; it was WRONG that the only practical effect is post-restart. Chain, established tonight and confirmed against the running qmake binary: `GarminTokenStore::loadChecked()` (`src/Cloud/GarminTokenStore.cpp`) returns `LoadStatus::Ok` WITH EMPTY BYTES for a 0-byte file at 0600 — the not-found guard and the REQ-006 permission guard BOTH pass it as healthy, so nothing downstream can tell an empty blob from a good one. `GarminConnect::open()` (`:485-490`) therefore proceeds to `blockingRestore("")`, which fails, and returns the REQ-005 silent-reauth failure. Critically, `CloudServiceSyncDialog`/`GarminConnect::open()` reauthenticate from `tokens.json` ONLY and NEVER reuse the live wizard session — so a user who has just connected successfully through the wizard IN THE SAME RUNNING APP SESSION still cannot sync. LIVE EVIDENCE (user-attended, real account): with `active-account.json` freshly written 0600 and the wizard connect succeeding, Share > Synchronise Activities... > Garmin Connect produced exactly `"Garmin Connect: could not restore the stored session; please sign in again."` (`GarminConnect.cpp:488`), and `~/.goldencheetah/Andy/activities/` gained ZERO files (still 1145, newest still `2026_08_18_19_13_47.json`) despite the user having recorded a brand-new Garmin activity specifically to exercise the path. Post-exit log lines 18-22 show four `gc_obs op=auth outcome=fail error_code=unknown` traces — the restore failures — which were invisible during the session itself (see [[B-STAGE9-12]]). Consequence for sequencing: this is the single defect standing between the project and a working end-to-end Stage 9 sync, and it outranks its own MFA sibling [[B-STAGE9-10]] in user impact. Secondary hardening question raised, NOT yet decided: whether `loadChecked()` treating a 0-byte file as `LoadStatus::Ok` is itself a defect worth a defence-in-depth guard, since the Python-side fix alone leaves that C++ acceptance of an empty blob unchanged. — original assessment retained below for the record — **OPEN, blocking for REQ-004 — NOT blocking live re-test attempt #5; both halves of that call now CONFIRMED BY LIVE EVIDENCE (attempt #5, 2026-09-13).** The connect succeeded end-to-end AND `~/.goldencheetah/Andy/config/garminconnect/tokens.json` was written **0 bytes, mode 0600**, exactly as this finding predicted: `dumps()` raises, `dump_tokens()` fails, the worker persists an empty blob, and auth is unaffected. The 0600 mode on the empty file confirms REQ-006's permission discipline is applied on the write path regardless of blob content. Practical effect on the user right now: Garmin Connect is configured and working, but the session is not restorable, so credentials must be re-entered after an app restart. Deliberately excluded from B-STAGE9-09's atomic unit (builder correctly flagged rather than fixed, staying inside its assigned scope). It cannot fail the connect itself: `PyEmbeddedAdapter.cpp:618-633` sets `out.kind = PyAuthOutcome::Success` BEFORE calling `dump_tokens`, then on any failure clears the pending error and leaves `tokenBlob` empty, under an explicit contract comment ("A dump failure must NOT fail an otherwise-successful auth ... the worker treats empty as 'nothing to persist'"). That deliberate degrade-don't-fail design, made for REQ-004, is what keeps this defect off the critical path for attempt #5 — the user will connect successfully but will have to re-enter credentials after a restart until this is fixed. Queue order for `garmin_builder_stage9_v3` after B-STAGE9-09 lands: this and [[B-STAGE9-10]] together are the remaining DEC-014 OQ1 residuals and should be dispatched as ONE unit with a shared real-wheel signature/contract test extending `test_adapter_real_library_contract.py` (the guard B-STAGE9-09 introduced), since both are the same root cause and both need the same class of executable check — an existence check is insufficient for the MFA half, whose defect is argument ORDER. | B-STAGE9-12 | Stage 9 live sync re-test (2026-09-13, user-attended), while chasing why a sync downloaded nothing | blocking | **REQ-NF-Obs-001's structured `gc_obs` trace lines are effectively invisible for live troubleshooting in the real qmake binary, and arrive out of order when they do arrive.** `gcObsTrace()` (`src/Cloud/GarminConnect.cpp:72-80`) emits one `gc_obs op=... outcome=... error_code=... duration_ms=...` line via `qDebug()` on every `open()` and every `readdir()` call, success and every early-return guard alike — the observability that DES-008/REQ-NF-Obs-001 exists to provide. `main.cpp`'s `nostderr()` (`src/Core/main.cpp:173-182`) redirects stderr with `freopen(file, "w", stderr)`. stderr's standard unbuffered property is NOT preserved across `freopen` onto a regular file: glibc gives the reopened stream full (block) buffering. GoldenCheetah's own `myMessageOutput` handler writes via `fprintf(stderr, ...)` and therefore accumulates in a ~4KB stdio buffer, while the embedded Python adapter writes to fd 2 DIRECTLY and lands immediately. Two consequences, both observed live tonight: (a) during a whole app session of Garmin troubleshooting the log showed only the Python-side `429` warnings and ZERO `gc_obs` lines, which actively misled diagnosis — the absence was read as 'the code path never executed' when in fact it had executed four times; (b) on clean exit the buffer flushed and all four lines appeared at once, INTERLEAVED OUT OF ORDER with the fd-2 writes (a startup Wayland-plugin line landed *after* the 429 pairs that chronologically followed it), so the log cannot be read as a timeline. Evidence: `~/.goldencheetah/goldencheetah.log` lines 18-22 after exit — `gc_obs op=auth outcome=fail error_code=unknown duration_ms=31/0/39/48` — none of which were present while the app ran. Note the secondary signal in that evidence: all four carry `error_code=unknown`, i.e. the empty-token-blob restore of [[B-STAGE9-11]] folds to `GarminRestoreFailure::Unknown` rather than a distinct kind — the same undiagnosable-Unknown fold class as [[B-STAGE9-09]] | **FIXED — closure at the end of this cell. NOT COMMITTED. THE HARD HOLD REMAINS IN FORCE.** RED deliverable: `unittests/Core/stderrbuf/{CMakeLists.txt,nostderr_mirror.cpp,test_stderr_buffering.py}` (new, untracked) plus a 6-line `add_subdirectory(Core/stderrbuf)` registration in `unittests/CMakeLists.txt`. `src/Core/main.cpp` verified CLEAN — the hold held. `nostderr()` cannot be linked into a test binary (it owns `main()`, a `QApplication` and the whole GUI dependency graph, and extracting it would mean editing the held file), so the builder wrote a hand-copied POSIX mirror of its `freopen`→`fileno`→`dup2`→`sync_with_stdio` sequence and drove it from a ctest-registered Python driver asserting both live symptoms. **`garmin_inspector_v1_20` INDEPENDENTLY CONFIRMED THE TEST DISCRIMINATES** rather than accepting the builder's report: built the mirror standalone with `g++ -std=c++17` into a `mktemp -d` dir (no project build tree, no qmake, nothing that could race the held binary), then ran the driver twice — with `GC_STDERR_MIRROR_APPLY_FIX` CLEARED it emitted `FAIL liveness` and `FAIL ordering: expected chronological [A, B, C], got ['B', 'A', 'C']` (exit 1), and with `GC_STDERR_MIRROR_APPLY_FIX=1` it emitted `PASS liveness` / `PASS ordering` (exit 0). The `['B','A','C']` interleaving reproduces this finding's live out-of-order symptom exactly. It is not a fake agreeing with the assumption. **REVIEWER ROUND 1 — 2 BLOCKING FOR COMMITTING THE RED DELIVERY:** (1) *the ambient environment can turn the registered RED test GREEN while `main.cpp` stays unfixed* — `nostderr_mirror.cpp:50` applies the `setvbuf` fix itself when `GC_STDERR_MIRROR_APPLY_FIX=1`, and the ctest registration (`unittests/Core/stderrbuf/CMakeLists.txt:33`) neither clears nor overrides that variable, so any developer or CI shell exporting it makes ctest report success over the unfixed defect. **The Inspector had independently identified this hazard in the review brief and then PROVED it empirically in the run above — the `GC_STDERR_MIRROR_APPLY_FIX=1` leg IS that exploit.** This is a test that can pass either way, the exact failure class behind [[B-STAGE9-09]]/[[B-STAGE9-10]]/[[B-STAGE9-11]]. (2) *a deliberately-failing test is registered into every Unix test run* — `unittests/CMakeLists.txt:11` adds the subdirectory unconditionally and `unittests/Core/stderrbuf/CMakeLists.txt:27` registers a plain `add_test` with neither `DISABLED` nor `WILL_FAIL`; LABELS do not exclude a test from plain `ctest`. So committing this as-is turns the WHOLE project suite red until the held production fix lands, breaking the pre-commit gate and every other agent's green run — including the parallel [[B-STAGE9-13]] unit. **REMEDY DECIDED by `garmin_inspector_v1_20` per the autonomy-boundary technical-decision rule:** remove the `GC_STDERR_MIRROR_APPLY_FIX` toggle from the registered mirror OUTRIGHT (its only purpose was a one-time discrimination check, which both the builder and the Inspector have now performed and recorded here — keeping it buys nothing and costs a permanent false-green channel); and register the test with `DISABLED TRUE` rather than `WILL_FAIL TRUE`. `WILL_FAIL` was scored and REJECTED because it makes the suite ASSERT the defect — it would go red at the moment the fix lands, i.e. it certifies the bug, which is this project's signature failure mode; `DISABLED` keeps the characterization test in-tree and discoverable, keeps `ctest` green so the gate and the parallel unit are unblocked, and the flip to enabled is the same edit that lands the fix in `main.cpp` and mirrors it. **REVIEWER ROUND 1 — 2 BLOCKING FOR THE GREEN FIX (not for this RED commit):** (3) *mirror drift has only a comment guard* — `nostderr_mirror.cpp:8` claims a "byte-for-byte copy" but already omits `main.cpp`'s `qDebug()` calls and replaces its error paths with `perror`; nothing fails if the production redirect sequence changes. Required before green: an EXECUTABLE source-contract check that reads `src/Core/main.cpp:173` and verifies the `freopen`→`fileno`→`dup2`→`sync_with_stdio`→`setvbuf` sequence AND placement — the same contract-test remedy pattern this project adopted for [[B-STAGE9-09]]/[[B-STAGE9-10]]/[[B-STAGE9-11]] (`test_adapter_real_library_contract.py`). Inspector constraint added at dispatch: write that check so it is drift-proof in BOTH directions — assert the fix-independent ordering separately from the presence/absence of the `setvbuf` call, so it does not itself need rewriting the moment the fix lands. (4) *the decided `_IOLBF` remedy does not solve Windows* — `nostderr()` has a separate `#ifdef WIN32` branch (`src/Core/main.cpp:206`, `_get_osfhandle` + `SetStdHandle`) that the mirror deliberately excludes, and Microsoft documents `_IOLBF` as EQUIVALENT TO FULL BUFFERING on Win32, so option (a) restores neither liveness nor ordering there. The builder flagged this unprompted before the review and the reviewer confirmed it against primary Microsoft documentation — two independent sources, so it is accepted as established. **AMENDMENT TO THE DECIDED REMEDY, made by `garmin_inspector_v1_20`:** option (a) stands for POSIX, but is now explicitly scoped as POSIX-COMPLETE / WINDOWS-INCOMPLETE, and the green unit must carry a Windows companion (`_IONBF` on the WIN32 branch, or an equivalent) rather than shipping a fix that is silently a no-op on one platform. This belongs beside the production implementation and in this ledger row, NOT only in a test CMakeLists comment where no Windows maintainer would find it. **REVIEWER ROUND 1 — 3 NON-BLOCKING driver issues, all to be folded into the green round:** `test_stderr_buffering.py:36` never checks the liveness child's exit status, so a failed `freopen` produces the expected RED result for the WRONG reason; the fixed `time.sleep(1)` at `:37` can false-FAIL on a severely delayed process start (poll for the marker until a deadline shorter than the child's `sleep(2)` instead); and the ordering parser at `:53` keys on any line merely STARTING with A/B/C, so a sanitizer or runtime diagnostic line could contaminate the sequence, while a missing ordering log raises instead of producing the driver's clean failure message. Reviewer also narrowed one of the builder's claims: Qt documents only that its default handler writes to stderr on Unix — the stronger claim that BOTH the default handler and `myMessageOutput` specifically use `fprintf(stderr, ...)` is not established by that documentation for the default handler, so the GREEN evidence must include a Qt-path check or the live retest rather than resting on it. The delivered fix set `setvbuf(stderr, nullptr, _IOLBF, 0)` unconditionally at `:188` and added an `_IONBF` companion at `:209`, inside the WIN32 `SetStdHandle` block. On Win32 MSVC defines `_IOLBF` as full buffering, so `:188` reinstates the very defect [[B-STAGE9-12]] exists to remove; `:209` then runs AFTER `fileno(stderr)`, `dup2` and `sync_with_stdio`, and C11 7.21.5.6p2 permits `setvbuf` only before any other operation on the stream, so it may legitimately be refused. Both calls discard their return value, so a refusal is silent. The contract check `test_main_cpp_contract.py:72-79` accepts the companion ANYWHERE inside the `#ifdef WIN32` block, and `nostderr_mirror.cpp` is Unix-only, so no registered test can distinguish a working Windows fix from a refused one — the pass-either-way class of [[B-STAGE9-09]]/[[B-STAGE9-10]]/[[B-STAGE9-11]]. Reviewer's own qualification, adopted: Microsoft's documented precondition is narrower ("no I/O since opened") and does not by itself establish `fileno` as I/O; C11's stream-operation rule is the sufficient ground, and the unchecked result leaves the Windows outcome unverified either way. REMEDY DECIDED by `garmin_inspector_v1_31` per the autonomy-boundary technical-decision rule (an ordinary implementation call): ONE `setvbuf` call in `nostderr()`, at the only legal position immediately after the freopen NULL-check, its mode selected by `#ifdef WIN32` (`_IONBF`) / `#else` (`_IOLBF`), its return value checked with a `qDebug` on failure like every other failure path in that function. The contract check must assert exactly-one-call, placement, `#ifdef`-selected mode, and result-checked as SEPARATE failure entries, with the existing call-ORDER assertion independent of all of them. Windows keeps no executable liveness coverage; that limit is accepted and belongs in this row, not in a test comment. Round 13's proposed rule therefore INVERTS the default — a line whose first non-empty content is a bullet marker must strictly parse as an Arms bullet or raise `ArmsBulletMalformed`, and the silent-skip branch is REMOVED for bullet-shaped lines, so no character class is load-bearing at all. Two FTDI pins (`appveyor/macos/install.sh:40` D2XX1.4.24, `appveyor/linux/install.sh:21` libftd2xx-1.4.27) return 403 on HEAD and are explicitly NOT classified — HEAD-only evidence cannot tell a dead asset from a hotlink block, and the investigator declined to guess. **FIX DIRECTION DECIDED 2026-09-20 (`garmin_inspector_v1_45`, after `B-STAGE9-42-fix-scope`): PIN-TO-A-LIVE-VERSION, not an API resolver.** The investigator's measured API-lookup replacement was reviewed and REJECTED on three blocking grounds, all against a RELEASE recipe: (a) `appveyor/linux/install.sh:4-54` provisions wget but never curl or jq, so the filter has an unproven dependency at that point in the script; (b) the upstream tag rotates its selected patch, so identical source revisions would fetch different CPython builds with no review, checksum or lockfile change — the present 404 is at least loud and diagnosable; (c) without `pipefail` plus an explicit non-empty check, a rate-limit JSON body yields an empty URL and `wget ""` fails opaquely under `set -e`. Decided repair: pin the version to the measured-live 3.11.16 AND add a hard-coded SHA-256 verification immediately after download and before extraction, failing loudly on absence or mismatch — `appveyor.yml:258`'s existing shasum covers the OUTPUT AppImage only, so input integrity is unchecked today. An API-provided digest would not be an independent trust anchor. Non-blocking and verified: no downstream path hard-codes `3.11.14`; only the local filename does, and `PYTHON_VERSION=3.11`/cp311 stay ABI-aligned with 3.11.16. Superseded proposal, kept for the record: resolve the asset from the release tag's API listing rather than a literal filename (`curl -fsSL https://api.github.com/repos/niess/python-appimage/releases/tags/python${PYTHON_VERSION}` filtered to the `cp<ver>-cp<ver>-manylinux_2_28_x86_64.AppImage` asset), which selects current 3.11.16 and HEADs 200 — the upstream tag rotates its assets, which is the actual mechanism. `appveyor/linux/after_build.sh:5` pins `PYTHON_APPIMAGE_VERSION=3.11.14`, consumed at `:43-46` to fetch the python3.11 AppImage. The upstream release tag replaces its assets rather than keeping them, so that exact URL now returns HTTP 404 and the pinned version is unexecutable. CLASS: cached-origin validation accepts a non-file as provenance — NEW. **ROUND 5 (`B-STAGE9-38-u3-r5-review`, 2026-09-20): still NOT-CLOSED.** The builder dropped the `cleanFile == cleanDir` disjunct and added `QFileInfo::isFile()`, which does close the exact reported input (proven RED against the restored line, isolated to one test). The reviewer then found the next shape: `__file__` set to a REAL file under the override, e.g. `<override>/gc_garmin_adapter/__init__.py`, with `__path__` pointing at a decoy package elsewhere — `moduleFileIsUnderDir` passes and `gc_garmin_adapter.garmin_client` then resolves through the decoy `__path__`. CLASS: cached-origin validation accepts a forged existing file path without validating the cached package search path — NEW (third consecutive NEW class, so the mechanical same-class counter is still 0). **ESCALATED by the Inspector rather than repaired again.** Three rounds have each closed the named attribute and surfaced the next one, and every attribute the predicate can read is settable by whoever seeded the entry — the rabbit-hole shape the repair-round gate describes, transposed from prose recognition to Python attributes. User-reproduced: cancelled, retried, the activity never reappeared. The name `recordImported` encodes the confusion — the record means "downloaded", and DES-009's resumability contract is what it actually serves. | open — BUILDER-READY.
